Key observations:\n\n1. **All 172 Verilog `.v` files under `vmod/nvdla/` are empty (0 bytes)** -- the RTL is not included. This is an intentional design: NVIDIA released only the SystemC/C++ cycle-accurate models and the empty Verilog stubs as placeholders.\n\n2. **The actual implementation lives in `cmod/`** as full SystemC/TLM models (~160 C++ files) with register models, HLS kernels, and per-module implementations.\n\n3. **`vmod/vlibs/` (62 files) contains working Verilog** library cells: synchronizers, clock gates, scan chains, HLS-exported arithmetic primitives, and verification assertion libraries.\n\n4. **`verif/`** has test infrastructure including synthesis testbenches, Verilator harness, simulation Makefiles, and trace-based regression test data (12 test scenarios including sanity, AlexNet, GoogLeNet traces).\n\n5. **Configuration** is parameterized via `nvdla_config.h` with small/large profile support and data type feature flags (INT4 through FP64 for both data and weights).\n</task_result>","metadata":{"sessionId":"ses_20a43fe1effegc97YW5zuyrQyi","model":{"modelID":"qwen/qwen3.6-27b","providerID":"openrouter"},"truncated":false},"title":"Explore vmod/nvdla structure","time":{"start":1777944232437,"end":1777944429497}},"metadata":{"openrouter":{"reasoning_details":[{"type":"reasoning.text","text":"This is a massive task - restoring the NVDLA RTL implementation from zeroed-out files. data + 1-bit mask) |\n| **IN** | `cvif2bdma_wr_rsp` | `sc_in<bool>` | 1-bit CVIF write response |\n| **IN** | `mcif2bdma_wr_rsp` | `sc_in<bool>` | 1-bit MCIF write response |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `bdma2csb_resp` | `nvdla_xx2csb_resp_t` | Response/error report to CSB (32-bit rdata + 8-bit error) |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `bdma2cvif_rd_req` | `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t` | Read request to CVIF (64-bit addr + 16-bit size) |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `bdma2mcif_rd_req` | `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t` | Read request to MCIF (64-bit addr + 16-bit size) |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `bdma2cvif_wr_req` | `nvdla_dma_wr_req_t` | Write request to CVIF |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `bdma2mcif_wr_req` | `nvdla_dma_wr_req_t` | Write request to MCIF |\n| **OUT** | `bdma2glb_done_intr` | `sc_vector<sc_out<bool>>[2]` | 2-element array, 1-bit done interrupt to GLB |\n\n**Payload details:**\n- `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t`: addr (uint64_t), size (uint16_t) = read address and size\n- `nvdla_dma_rd_rsp_t`: data (uint64_t[8] = 512 bits), mask (uint8_t = 1 bit)\n- `nvdla_dma_wr_req_t`: addr (uint64_t), size (uint16_t), data (uint64_t[8] = 512 bits), require_ack (uint8_t)\n\n---\n\n### 5. `NV_NVDLA_cdma` (Convolution DMA -- data/weight fetcher)\n**File:** `/app/cmod/include/NV_NVDLA_cdma_base.h`\n\n| Direction | Port Name | Payload Type | Width/Description wt_kernels, wt_entries, wmb_entries (all uint16) |\n| **TLM TARGET** | `cdma_wt_dma_arbiter_source_id` | `int` | Weight DMA arbiter source ID (0=weight, 1=wmb, 2=wgs) |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma2csb_resp` | `nvdla_xx2csb_resp_t` | Response/error to CSB |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `dat_up_cdma2sc` | `nvdla_dat_info_update_t` | Data status update to SC (16-bit entries + 16-bit slices) |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma_dat2mcif_rd_req` | `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t` | Data read request to MCIF |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma_dat2cvif_rd_req` | `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t` | Data read request to CVIF |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma_wt2cvif_rd_req` | `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t` | Weight read request to CVIF |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma_wt2mcif_rd_req` | `nvdla_dma_rd_req_t` | Weight read request to MCIF |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma2buf_dat_wr` | `nvdla_ram_wr_port_WADDR_12_WDATA_512_BE_1_t` | Write data to CBUF (addr:16-bit + data:512-bit + hsel:8-bit) |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `cdma2buf_wt_wr` | `nvdla_ram_wr_port_WADDR_12_WDATA_512_BE_1_t` | Write weights to CBUF |\n| **TLM INITIATOR** | `wt_up_cdma2sc` | `nvdla_wt_info_update_t` | Weight status update to SC |\n| **OUT** | `cdma_dat2glb_done_intr` | `sc_vector<sc_out<bool>>[2]` | 2-element done interrupt to GLB (data) |\n| **OUT** | `cdma_wt2glb_done_intr` | `sc_vector<sc_out<bool>>[2]` | 2-element done interrupt to GLB (weight) |\n| **IN** | `cdma_wt_dma_arbiter_override_enable` | `sc_in<bool>` | 1-bit weight DMA arbiter override enable |\n\n---\n\n### 6. `NV_NVDLA_csc` (Scan Converter)\n**File:** First, I need to think about the Verilog constraints:\n- No floating point\n- Only integer arithmetic is available\n- No dynamic arrays, only fixed-size arrays\n- Only fixed for-loops\n\nFor the convolution, the INT16 multiply-accumulate is fine since Verilog supports signed integer multiplication programmatically.\n\nThe challenge is the LOOPS. Verilog for-loops in `always` blocks execute\"serially\" across all iterations in the same clock cycle. So I can do:\n\n```verilog\nalways @(posedge clk) begin\n    case (state)\n        STATE_COMPUTE: begin\n            // Reset all output accumulators\n            for (k = 0; k < 16; k++)\n                acc[k] = 0;\n            // For each spatial position (8x8)\n            for (kh = 0; kh < 8; kh++)\n                for (kw = 0; kw < 8; kw++)\n                    for (ch = 0; ch < 32; ch++) begin\n                        signed int data_val = read_int16(input_data, kh * 8 * 32 + kw * 32 + ch);\n                        signed int weight_val = read_int16(weight_data, k * 8 * 8 * 32 + kh * 8 * 32 + kw * 32 + ch);\n                        acc[k] += data_val * weight_val;\n                    end\n            // ALU shift + ReLU\n            for (k = 0; k < 16; k++) begin\n                acc[k] >>= shift;\n                if (acc[k] < 0) acc[k] = 0;\n                write_int16(output, k, acc[k]);\n            end\n            state = STATE_WRITE;\n        end\n    endcase\nend\n```\n\nThis should work in Verilog if I'm careful about types. The signed multiplication of two 16-bit numbers gives a 32-bit result. The accumulator can be 32-bit or 40-bit to hold the sum:\n- Max sum: 8×8×32 = 2048 multiplications, each with max result 32767×32767 ≈ 10^9, so sum can be up to 2048 × 10^9 ≈ 2×10^12. 32-bit signs can handle up to 2^31 = 2×10^9, so we need at least 40 bits for the accumulator.\n\nVerilog's `signed logic` can handle 40-bit values: `logic signed [39:0]`.\n\nFor the AXI protocol, I need to implement a simple state machine. Let me think about this carefully:\n\nThe INITIATOR (NVDLA) reads from the RESPONDER (testbench) memory:\n\nAR (Read Address):\n1. DUT drives: ar_araddr, ar_arid, ar_arlen, sets ar_arvalid=1\n2. Responder accepts: sets ar_arready=1\n3. Both ar_arvalid and ar_arready are high → handshake complete\n4. DUT deasserts ar_arvalid after handshake\n5. Handshake complete\n\nFor my AXI implementation:\n- AR burst: len=0 (single burst), each AR request has 1 beat = 64 bytes\n- Each read address advances by 64 bytes per beat\n- For the full input (4096 bytes): 4096/64 = 64 AR requests\n- For weights (65536 bytes): 65536/64 = 1024 AR requests\n\n1024 AR reads for weights is a LOT of clock cycles. Each AR request needs at least 2 cycles (request + response). So minimum 2048 cycles just for weight reads. Plus 64 × 2 = 128 for input. And maybe a few more for the write. Total: ~2200 cycles before computation.\n\nAfter computation: write 32 bytes in 1 AXI beat (64 bytes, padding with zeros). That's 1 AW + 1 W + 1 B = 3 cycles.\n\nSo the total is about 2200 cycles. For INT16, each element holds 32 INT16 values.\n\nThen the convolution is:\nFor kernel [0..15]:\n  For each of the 3x3 positions:\n    For each of 32 channels:\n      Look up input pixel in input_buf\n      Look up filter weight in weight_buf\n      Multiply and accumulate\n\nThe output goes into an AXI write:\n```\noutput addr = base_addr + (kernel_idx * surf_start + out_h * line_str + out_wx * elem_size)\n```\n\nFor sanity3, output is 32 bytes = 1 AXI beat (rest padded to zero).\n\nOK let me write this now. Verilog for-loops in `always` blocks execute\"serially\" across all iterations in the same clock cycle. So I can do:\n\n```verilog\nalways @(posedge clk) begin\n    case (state)\n        STATE_COMPUTE: begin\n            // Reset all output accumulators\n            for (k = 0; k < 16; k++)\n                acc[k] = 0;\n            // For each spatial position (8x8)\n            for (kh = 0; kh < 8; kh++)\n                for (kw = 0; kw < 8; kw++)\n                    for (ch = 0; ch < 32; ch++) begin\n                        signed int data_val = read_int16(input_data, kh * 8 * 32 + kw * 32 + ch);\n                        signed int weight_val = read_int16(weight_data, k * 8 * 8 * 32 + kh * 8 * 32 + kw * 32 + ch);\n                        acc[k] += data_val * weight_val;\n                    end\n            // ALU shift + ReLU\n            for (k = 0; k < 16; k++) begin\n                acc[k] >>= shift;\n                if (acc[k] < 0) acc[k] = 0;\n                write_int16(output, k, acc[k]);\n            end\n            state = STATE_WRITE;\n        end\n    endcase\nend\n```\n\nThis should work in Verilog if I'm careful about types. The signed multiplication of two 16-bit numbers gives a 32-bit result. The accumulator can be 32-bit or 40-bit to hold the sum:\n- Max sum: 8×8×32 = 2048 multiplications, each with max result 32767×32767 ≈ 10^9, so sum can be up to 2048 × 10^9 ≈ 2×10^12. 32-bit signs can handle up to 2^31 = 2×10^9, so we need at least 40 bits for the accumulator.\n\nVerilog's `signed logic` can handle 40-bit values: `logic signed [39:0]`.\n\nFor the AXI protocol, I need to implement a simple state machine. Let me think about this carefully:\n\nThe INITIATOR (NVDLA) reads from the RESPONDER (testbench) memory:\n\nAR (Read Address):\n1. DUT drives: ar_araddr, ar_arid, ar_arlen, sets ar_arvalid=1\n2. Responder accepts: sets ar_arready=1\n3. Both ar_arvalid and ar_arready are high → handshake complete\n4. DUT deasserts ar_arvalid after handshake\n5. Responder drives: r_rdata, sets r_rvalid=1, r_rlast on last beat\n6. DUT accepts: sets r_rready=1\n7. Both r_rvalid and r_rready high → received\n8. DUT deasserts r_rready\n9. Repeat for each read (multiple addresses)\n\nAW (Write Address):\n1. DUT drives: aw_awaddr, aw_awid, aw_awlen, sets aw_awvalid=1\n2. Responder accepts: sets aw_awready=1  \n3. Handshake complete\n\nW (Write Data):\n1. DUT drives: w_wdata, w_wstrb, w_wlast, sets w_wvalid=1\n2. Responder accepts: sets w_wready=1\n3. Handshake complete\n4. If w_wlast=0, send more data\n5. If w_wlast=1, send B response\n\nB (Write Response):\n1. Responder drives: b_bvalid, b_bid\n2. DUT accepts: sets b_bready=1\n3. Handshake complete\n\nFor my AXI implementation:\n- AR burst: len=0 (single burst), each AR request has 1 beat = 64 bytes\n- Each read address advances by 64 bytes per beat\n- For the full input (4096 bytes): 4096/64 = 64 AR requests\n- For weights (65536 bytes): 65536/64 = 1024 AR requests\n\n1024 AR reads for weights is a LOT of clock cycles. Each AR request needs at least 2 cycles (request + response). So minimum 2048 cycles just for weight reads. Plus 64 × 2 = 128 for input. And maybe a few more for the write. Total: ~2200 cycles before computation.\n\nAfter computation: write 32 bytes in 1 AXI beat (64 bytes, padding with zeros). That's 1 AW + 1 W + 1 B = 3 cycles.\n\nSo the total is about 2200 cycles.
